# Notebook

Ideas, in a line or two, dated. The owner's in her words; ours marked "ours". An idea here may be tried in the
sandbox at once and reaches a registered run, a paper or the programme page only by promotion (CLAUDE.md rules 9
and 16). Earlier ideas are in `VISION.md`, Updates 16 to 54.

- 2026-10-11, ours. What makes a flat torus widen? In T48, with the released energy kept in place, 6 of 15 did.
- 2026-10-11, ours. Do the two possible twists, one each way, meeting along a tube that opened from two places,
  leave a knot?

- 2026-10-11, ours. Three ways a small direction could grow in a toy like this: the burp's own heat breaks up the
  new tube and it re-forms wide; the rule gains a price for longer loops, so that wider is cheaper; or growth is a
  separate, slow stage driven by counting, which needs the space to stay warm enough to rearrange.

- 2026-10-11, ours, what that idea touches (general knowledge, not read by us, to verify before any of it is
  cited). (1) Along a light ray the distance in space and the lapse of time cancel exactly: its length in
  spacetime is zero and a clock riding on it would not tick. (2) In a light wave the electric and magnetic parts
  cancel in both of the combinations every observer agrees on. (3) Kaluza and Klein: light as the part of gravity
  that points along one extra, curled direction, with electric charge as motion round it. (4) In quantum mechanics
  the wave of two particles lives in a space of six dimensions and of three particles in nine, three per
  particle, and identical particles are not told apart there until something interacts with them. (5) Penrose's
  twistors take light rays as the basic points; the light rays of our spacetime form a five-dimensional space
  inside a six-dimensional one. (6) Feynman's sum over paths: every path counts, each weighted by its length
  measured in wavelengths, and probabilities come from the sum: count-like, length-of-loop-like and time-like at
  once. The record's caution stands beside it: counting the versions of one fixed arrangement cannot reproduce the
  quantum correlations (T15 rung 3a); a view from more dimensions is a different proposal and has no test here yet.

- 2026-10-11, the owner. "OK note that energy as heat does that energy is curling energy or another type in our
  interesting runs". Ours, bookkeeping, exact in the toy: there is one energy, held in three places. In the curl
  (4(λ − 1) per point of a curled tube). In broken squares (16 each). In the reservoir, as heat. The burp is curl
  energy becoming heat. The push that starts it is a little heat gathered in one place, lent and given back. The
  reverse, heat becoming curl, has not happened in any run (T21, T26, T27, T34, T57, and the looks in the log).
- 2026-10-11, ours. Why heat does not curl fabric, to check with a physicist: heat is energy with no direction,
  and there are far more broken arrangements than curled ones for it to land in. Curling needs energy delivered
  along one route: work, not heat. The toy has no way to do work on a direction. One that it could be given: a
  store that pays only for the moves of one chosen column, a piston.
- 2026-10-11, ours. With a price on loops of six and one push, the first finished end states were one-sided
  (Klein bottles). Why? One push makes one defect, not a pair; does a lone defect that runs the length of the tube
  close the seam with a flip? Would two pushes, or a push of the other kind, give a torus?

- 2026-10-12, the owner, on the sealed runs labelled "melted": "can we do some tests on the idea what if melting
  isnt allowed the connections return to connecting locally close points pretty quick?" Ours, two readings to
  put to her before anything is registered (a new rule either way, S1). A hard one: a link may only be re-joined
  between points that are already near each other, so no link ever reaches across the network. A soft one: a
  long reach is allowed but costs energy in proportion to how far it reaches, so it snaps back. Where it would
  bite: the patchworks of T61, the flawed wide products of T62 and T64, the breakage of T34 and T57, and the
  long-range way out of the tube six around (O124). First a sandbox look.

- 2026-10-12, ours, the answer (`docs/theory/orh_findings.md`, section 10): the loop's measure must be Born-weighted; the view from outside is the decoherence functional of a closed system; a loop that turns for ever needs a record-erasing turn boundary, and the toy can run two turns with the records erased between them against kept. Not registered.
- 2026-10-13, ours, from reading the Chern group's papers (`docs/reading/notes/2026-10-13_chern_group_vocabulary.md`).
  (a) The column and the knots of O107 have nearly the same first-exit cost (12 and 11) and only the column
  anneals. Price the least possible highest point on any route from each to flat (their "minimax barrier"), by
  listing routes at a small size. The same calculation is what T54 still lacks (O118: patch energies, not
  barriers). (b) The question of 11 October above, two twists meeting, is in their words whether the product has
  two mirror-image versions with a wall where they meet; a joint between two widths (O116, 12 October) is a wall
  between two different members. Can a joint be removed while its two stretches keep different widths? (c) A
  point at d = 1 has a square too many and a point at d = 3 one too few; the knot of two kinds of point may be a
  bound pair of opposite flaws. In crystals a tube changes its width at such a pair (general knowledge, to
  verify). (d) The outcomes of T9 and T61 against room and curling cost, drawn as one map: their "dynamical
  phase diagram".

  currently does? would including or running both help our paper 1 and our programme more generally? especially

  question for the scrap: cool two sets of finished tubes to the same cold bath over the same time, one set

- 2026-10-13, the owner, reading the plain-language edition of paper 1: "Flat" in its descriptions and diagrams
  is misleading; "we need to state that the tube is a ring and the flat is a fatter doughnut"; "these are not
  truly flat in the open direction way ... they are bigger doughnuts". Her question: "is there a thought
  experiment, a way they are like flat and from some perspective are open direction? because if you cannot walk
  through squares no one could be inside the 4 tube, but they can be inside the bigger doughnut". *Ours, exact:*
  nothing in any toy of the programme is open-ended, at four, six or eight links. A finite network in which
  every point has all its links has no edge, so every arrangement closes on itself, and "opening a direction"
  has only ever meant its way around becoming longer than one square. Flat (every link on exactly two squares)
  is about a point's surroundings; wide is about the whole. Her view from inside is the `width` reading already
  in the record (`scripts/read_endpoint_width.py`): the number of points within r steps matches the endless
  grid's 2r² + 2r + 1 until r is half the way around. So a torus W around is open to anything that can check
  fewer than W/2 steps, and the tube falls short at two steps, as early as a count can. Nobody is inside the
  tube's hollow, which is only in the drawing; what the tube lacks is any room along its curled direction.
  *Published, abstract only ([Planck15topo]):* astronomers put the same question to real space, which is
  measured flat and not measured endless. In the edition's Chapter 3 from 13 October.
- 2026-10-13, the owner, on the plain-language edition of paper 1: it "doesn't need to include sections that
  don't tell us important information about the next questions that we're gonna ask or help explain something
  about what the model says with support or against"; the pages on the long waits being "a measurement issue"
  are "just not necessary for this version of the paper"; its reader is "a lay person but somebody who really
  enjoys math physics and they want to understand how reality works and this theory", who does not need "how
  we got to where we got to unless it's part of the narrative". *Ours:* the technical edition keeps the full
  account, and the plain one keeps one sentence saying three earlier maps stay inconclusive, so that nothing
  inconvenient is hidden by the cut (rule 7).

- 2026-10-14, the owner, on the curling cost: it is masked where space is smooth, and "mass pays": "if there's too
  much energy or too much of a particular type in a particular concentration, it can curl space paying three
  dimensions at once. Okay it can be different force or mechanism than reg gravity". *Ours, exact:* H(lambda) =
  H(1) + 4(lambda - 1) X, and X = 0 on every graph with no edge on three squares (flat space; the Fig. 7
  background of [T25], O67), so off lambda = 1 the energy equals the published model's on every such graph; under
  "all at the last" curling one direction costs the same as three (O106). *Ours, general knowledge, to verify:*
  corrections to general relativity are expected to be silent at low curvature and act near black holes and the
  birth, which is the shape of a masked term.

  *Ours, reading it in the toy:* the fold is the burp run backwards. A wide flat torus is re-glued into the tube four
  around by a front from one or more seeds; curling energy pays for it and a relic seeds it; the black hole's inside is
  then the tube, paper 1's stand-in for X; a second activation opens it into a wide space again. If that reading is
  hers, the whole cycle lives in two directions and can be looked at now: `sandbox/feed_energy.py` with START twist and
  MODE curl, a relic planted first (the LOG of 11 October: with no relic, curl-only energy at 2 units per point moved
  nothing and at 8 per point melted the fabric). One exact point against "boom": in the toy curling takes energy in,
  4(lambda - 1) per point, and releases nothing, so a fold of k points can grow only as far as the curling energy
  supplied pays for, k = E / 4(lambda - 1); the release comes at the later opening. A "cool boom" that stores energy
  fits that; a fold that gives energy off does not. Questions put to her the same day, so that the design brief S1
  needs can be written from her answers:
  - *Piece 11, the fold.* (1) What carries curling energy: each relic, as its own stored curling cost (a column holds
    14 at lambda = 1.25), or stores at the relic filled from outside, or both? (2) What may a curl-only store pay for:
    only moves that add a surplus square without lowering the square count (strict), or any move that changes the
    curl even if it breaks squares too (the 11 October rule, which still melted at fast feeds)? (3) Is the relic
    required? A control with the same energy and no relic says whether it seeds. (4) Does the fold stop when the
    curling energy is spent, so that its size is set by the energy supplied and by nothing else? (5) Is the black
    hole's inside the tube four around, paper 1's X, so that the cycle closes in two directions; or does it need
    three? (6) What counts as a fold: a curled region attached to the relic that outlives the draining of the stores
    and is larger than T54's critical patch?
  - *Piece 5, the tie.* (7) Should a broken point (one with more open directions than the space has, or off the
    ladder) pay the tie as a curled point does, more, or nothing as now? The table as written charges it nothing, which
    is why T56's slab broke instead of opening. (8) Is the push to be supplied as a patch of several columns opened by
    hand (T54's critical patch), since a single move is not the barrier in three directions? (9) Is the slab still X,
    or is X now the relic-seeded tube of her fold, with the three-direction runs set aside as the untied ones were?
  - *Piece 7, the knots.* (10) Her prediction, in a word, for whether the double column and the knot of two kinds are
    still stuck at a curling cost of zero: STUCK, FREE, or one of each.

  lambda = 1.02, 1.10, 1.25 (O89). "Both ways": any of the three directions can curl, alike, by chance (O41); but in two
  directions and in three the toy has only ever uncurled, never curled from supplied energy (T26, T34, T57, the feed
  looks of 11 October), so "curl" is the half that needs her new ingredient. A relic's curling energy, exact (O126): a
  column costs 24 lambda - 16, of which 24(lambda - 1) is curling cost, 6 units at lambda = 1.25; curling a 256-point slab
  would take about 43 such relics' worth, a 192-point tube about 32. In six links no stable relic has been found (O58:

  yet; the two-direction cycle has, and is the one that can run now. "Stringification": the published word is
  compactification, a direction curled small beside large ones (Kaluza-Klein; string theory's extra dimensions), and
  the toy's four-around direction is its counterpart in shape only; the relatives for a direction opening or closing
  are the decompactification papers already anchoring paper 5 ([GM04], [CJR09], [BSV10], [GHR10]); the toy has no

  only curl, in two directions (LOG, same day). Visual: the artifact "The Curl and Snap Cycle", a sketch.

- 2026-10-15, evening, the owner, on the page's line that "one relic in each slice, looking like dark matter" rests on
  an object not found in three directions: "can this exist in 2d and not 3d? Doesn't interact with 3d?" *Ours, exact:*
  in the toy a slice is a set of points of the one six-link network, four links within the slice and two to the slices
  either side; the 2D column's re-wiring adds surplus squares in the slice and, once the points have their two
  between-slice links, breaks the squares those links were on, which is why it costs 120.5 in 3D instead of 14 and has
  a move downhill (O56). So "exists in 2D and not in 3D" means it survives only while the slice has no neighbours and
  dissolves once the slice is part of space. "Does not interact" is true of every defect at a distance (additive,
  O22, O56) and so cannot single the relic out; at contact a relic in a slice touches the slices either side through
  the squares its points sit on. For her picture the relic has to be a 3D object, a stuck re-wiring that includes the
  up and down links, not found yet (O58, searched narrowly). A rule in which slices are linked more weakly than points
  within a slice would be a different model and a new knob; its published relatives are the braneworld pictures
  (matter confined to a surface, only gravity crossing; *general knowledge, not read by us, to verify*). Energy beside
  a 2D column moved or dissolved it in 5 to 6 of 6 (LOG, 14 October), the toy's nearest thing to "collides".
- 2026-10-15, evening, the owner, on "the relic survives only while the slice has no neighbours": "this could be just in
  a black hole and the galaxies circling each other? We actually don't want to see it elsewhere?" *Ours.* In the toy
  this is self-consistent if the first listing finds no six-link relic: relics then live only in separate 2D slices,
  never in open 3D space. Its cost: the black hole's inside would have to be separate four-link slices, which is not
  the slab (six-link, one curled) and not a state the six-link toy can reach, since every point keeps six links; a fold
  that turns six links into four is a varying link count, parked in VISION, a new knob. *General knowledge, to
  verify:* as dark matter, "only inside black holes" fails on the observation that first revealed dark matter. Flat
  rotation curves need the extra mass spread through a halo far larger than the galaxy; a central mass gives orbits
  that slow with distance. The Milky Way's central black hole is about 4 × 10⁶ solar masses against a halo of about
  10¹²; the cosmic microwave background needs dark matter present and spread before any star or black hole; in
  colliding clusters the lensing mass sits apart from the galaxies' centres; dwarf galaxies are the most dark-matter
  dominated and have little or no central black hole. A relic inside a horizon counts from outside as black-hole mass
  only, so it is "feels gravity, nothing more" as she says, but it is black-hole mass, not the dark matter, unless
  black holes are the dark matter (primordial black holes, published and heavily constrained; the toy says nothing).

  change in the link count: it is a state of the six-link toy already. Against the tube: both are one connected piece

  (T56), so the rod's burp is half the tube's unless the second direction opens too. Its product is sometimes the rod

- 2026-10-15, evening, the owner: "Can the cosmic microwave background come from the vacuum of being a new space
  from within a knot?" *Ours; general knowledge, to verify:* not as a vacuum. The background is light with the most
  exact thermal spectrum ever measured (2.725 K), which needs a history of contact with hot matter; it was hotter
  earlier (read from distant gas clouds it warms) and cools as space grows, where a vacuum's energy would not; its
  ripples are sound waves in the hot gas, whose peaks fix the dark-to-ordinary ratio. The published temperature of an
  expanding space's vacuum (Gibbons and Hawking 1977, not read by us) is about 10⁻³⁰ K today. In the hypothesis the
  background is the surviving light of the burp (claim 4; Update 41's hot lump), and the burp's release per point
  would set the birth temperature, O89's one unknown, which the hypothesis does not yet give. The toy has no light,
  expansion or temperature but a bath's; its shape, one front from one seed leaving a uniform product with a sprinkle
  of fixed knots, is in kind what the sky shows (ours, unverified; the uniformity of a re-glued tube, not of a sky).

- 2026-10-16, the owner, on dark energy and the end state: "Dark energy is connected to inflation. I don't think that it
  changes over time. It's a feature in. From some perspective they are outside of time but hold time, so we know the
  start and the end (end of observers is call it 8) space is emergent, from the burp. In some ways, it is all
  happening inside a backdrop of the end state of the universe: which for this example we'll describe the point where
  the last black hole dissolves. Stable, flat, cold, similar in every universe, and another part of the theory says
  every version infinite versions exist. What would that say about what is going on in the background? Some universes
  are the same, we've talked about the way, as time passes, they move towards being 1 exact same thing, moving from
  the most variants to the least. Can something about that be a vacuum force? A background radiation? What else?"
  *Ours; general knowledge, not read for this entry, to verify with a physicist.* The published form of nearly all of
  it: a cosmological constant is a vacuum energy whose density never changes, and a universe that has one ends as de
  Sitter space, flat, cold, empty and expanding, whatever it started from (the cosmic no-hair theorem, Wald 1983),
  which is "many variants to one end state" as a theorem; that end state has a horizon with a thermal glow (Gibbons
  and Hawking 1977) at about 10⁻³⁰ K, which is the "background radiation" of the backdrop, real and far too faint to
  see; its "force" is the accelerated expansion itself, not a pull on history; inflation is the same kind of energy
  at about 10¹⁰⁰ times the density, which ended, and models that tie the two exist (quintessential inflation, Peebles
  and Vilenkin 1999). What no picture yet gives is the number, 10⁻¹²⁰ in natural units (Update 41 carries it openly).
  In her Everett line (O125) branches diverge and do not merge, so "becoming one exact same thing" is true of the
  macrostate every branch reaches, not of the branches. In the toy: no expansion, no time, the floor is exactly zero
  (Q18); its one counterpart is that thousands of runs end in the same arrangement (9,208 of 9,215, O116), the toy's
  version of an attractor. Not a knob, not a run; her position, recorded.
- 2026-10-16, the owner, on the beginning: "The rate of change is increasing at an increasing rate. So it used to be

  taken out (r = 2, 8, 14, 20, 28, 36 for the scraps and their sums) the smaller hits are 64 + 8 − 8 = 64 = 8²,
  64 + 8 − 36 = 36 = 6², 64 + 64 − 28 = 100 = 10². 576 is also the slab 4 × 12 × 12 (T65's L = 12 cells) and the
  rectangular flat tori 6 × 8 × 12 and 6 × 6 × 16, so the one size on her list that the toy already runs is the
  one it is running. *What the toy itself constrains (exact):* a fully curled piece is at most 64 points (O85); the
  count of points is fixed, so a 64-point knot alone can never be flat three-direction space (every side must be
  even and at least 6 to be flat, so the smallest rectangular flat torus is 6 × 6 × 6 = 216; twisted gluings exist and
  the smallest flat three-direction arrangement is not listed); "merging points" is the one thing never seen (O85:
  separate cubes opened and never joined). Her even-square condition is not a constraint of the toy; its meaning
  is hers to say (a square cross-section, as in the slab 4 × L × L, is the nearest the toy has). Rule 13: a size
  that matches is not a result. *General knowledge, to verify:* the expansion slowed for about nine billion years
  and has sped up since dark energy overtook matter about five billion years ago; under a constant vacuum energy the
  size grows ever faster while the rate per unit size settles to a constant, so "increasing at an increasing rate"
  is right for the size and not for the rate.

- 2026-10-16, the owner, correcting the arithmetic above: "I mean is it also an even cube not even square, why using
  square?" *Ours, exact:* 64 + m³ = k³ with m, k even has no solution (a sum of two cubes is never a cube; Euler's
  case of Fermat's last theorem, general knowledge). With relic points removed, 64 + m³ − r = k³ with r < 64 has only
  m = 2, k = 4, r = 8: the knot itself. If the whole space is to be a flat even cube (216, 512, 1,000), the remainder
  after the knot (152, 448, 936) is never a cube. Neither condition is one the toy imposes; what the condition stands
  for in her picture is hers to say.
- 2026-10-16, the owner: "That makes sense, it can be asymmetrical. The main point is check the dot counts compared to
  64 + m³ = L × W × H + that arrangement's relics. Is there a pattern? A minimum? A maximum?" *Ours, exact
  arithmetic:* with m even, N = 64 + m³ is a multiple of 8, and so is any flat rectangular box (even sides, each at
  least 6), so the relic remainder r = N − LWH is always a multiple of 8. An exact fit (r = 0) exists exactly when
  8 + (m/2)³ is a product of three whole numbers each at least 3. Over m = 2 to 40: no flat box fits below 216
  (m = 2, 4); m = 6 leaves 64 over the 6 × 6 × 6; the first exact fit is m = 8, N = 576 (6 × 6 × 16 and 6 × 8 × 12);
  then m = 12, 14, 16, 20, 24, 26, 28, 32, 34, 36, 38, 40 fit exactly, and the misses leave r = 8 (m = 10: 6 × 8 × 22
  plus one double column; m = 18), 32 (m = 22) and 16 (m = 30: a column and a pair). Minimum 576; no maximum, the
  exact fits grow denser with m. Two cautions: a flat torus need not be a rectangular box (twisted gluings exist,
  as the tube six around is at N = 64), so this is a sufficient condition, not the toy's rule; and the relic count
  is measured, about one per seed (T10, T51), not chosen to fill a remainder.

  energy cannot see (every curled direction costs the same per point, O41, O106) and the moves cannot see either

- 2026-10-16, the owner, on the fold: "Yes that makes sense re no micro selection of edge. But then does it need 3x
  curling energy to curl? What happens to energy that doesn't cause snap? Just stored in stores? Damage? Released
  back? When?" *Ours, from the record, no new run:* (1) Yes, under "all at the last" the first fold of flat space
  costs the whole 3a per point and the later two folds cost nothing (O106: every rung sits 3a above flat), so curling
  one direction is as dear as curling all three; untied it costs a per direction. The toy charges the energy, not a
  wall, for a fold: the single-move wall out of flat space is 64 either way. (2) In a sealed box energy that does not
  cause a snap has three homes and nothing else: the stores (the bath, or one point's store), broken squares
  (damage, 16 a square), and curled or opened structure; the sum is conserved to the last bit (Q12). It moves between
  them every sweep in both directions: a move that raises H draws from a store, a move that lowers H pays into one,
  so "released back" is continuous, not an event. (3) What was measured: concentrated energy went into damage, not
  curl (T57, O114: a fifth to a half of the space broken at one to two directions' worth, tied and untied); given a
  spark the tube's own release returns to the stores and the box ends with stores warm and the graph flat or
  flawed (T9); damage heals back into the stores when the stores can take it (T42, O82), and a push below the
  cheapest move (32 in 2D, 64 in 3D) sits in its store for ever and does nothing (Q22). Energy leaves the box only
  through a declared leak (the semi-permeable wall, Q12; T27 ran four rates). Not measured: how long damage
  takes to heal against store temperature in three directions (the measurement-side handoff's item 4 asks what
  the stores do).
- 2026-10-16, the owner, on the fold and on healing: "Energy additive across shared edge due to tie. Breaks curls or

  difference to healing (a damaged point pays no tie), and the three-direction space takes less damage at the same
  energy (its cheapest break costs 64 against 32) and heals to the scar level in about the same number of sweeps.

  bath (T48, T53). The published relative to read before citing is Poplawski (TASKS, 6 October item 6).

  universe space inside the black hole is about 640 at the start and cannot change, then how could the space that
  creates the black hole have over 640?" *Ours:* in the fixed-count toy one black hole's inside is always smaller
  than the universe that made it, so a burp inside one hole makes a daughter space and never the next turn (that
  is Smolin's tree, with N shrinking by generation). A loop with constant N needs her conjecture of 6 October: the
  turn ends when all N are inside black holes, and the next turn is all those insides opening together. Both can
  hold at once. The gap the record shows: for the reset to make one space, the many insides must be one connected
  X before the burp; from a gas of separate curled cubes no opening ever joined (O85), and seeds join only inside
  one connected X (T53). That is the foam question of 27 September, now load-bearing for the loop.

  function on the built slab: H = -576 + 720 = 144). Squares at +16 each with nothing against them is what healing a
  broken point gains, not what opening gains, and it is why flat space heals (T34, T66). Two other things in the sum:
  6 x 6 x 4 becoming 6 x 6 x 6 adds 72 points, which is her varying-count idea (parked; the toy keeps N fixed), and at

  three-direction form of what O116 found for the tube: it opens into something flat point by point whose shape is not a
  wider torus.

- 2026-10-18, the owner: "Maybe antimatter is curling energy used to uncurl, paying the threshold. Then matter is the
  released energy from the that is partly canceled by the threshold payment." *Ours, against the record:* (1) Its
  nearest home is Update 42's line on matter and antimatter: in standard physics the hot lump held almost equal amounts
  of each, they turned to light as it cooled, and ordinary matter is the one-part-in-a-billion excess; her idea makes
  the activation the antimatter and the release less the activation the matter, so the excess would be "release minus
  threshold". (2) Two facts stand against it as worded. Annihilation does not cancel energy: matter and antimatter meeting
  become light of the same energy, and what cancels is the particle count (baryon number), so "partly canceled" has to
  mean a count, not an energy. And in the toy a wall is a hill, not a toll (O71): the energy paid to cross comes back on
  the far side, so the threshold payment cancels nothing unless the paying energy is of a kind that is used up, which is
  what curling energy would be (Update 57) and which the strict rule of Update 60 cannot do, since that store pays only
  moves that add a surplus square without losing squares and uncurling loses squares. So the idea needs a store that may
  pay for uncurling and is spent in doing so, a third store rule beside the strict one and the no-break one of 18 October.
  (3) The toy has nothing that comes in opposite kinds (Update 42). The one signed pair it does have: a point's departure
  from flat is either a missing square (damage, 16 each) or a surplus square (curl, 4(λ − 1) each), and a switch can trade
  one for the other (the opening move loses 2 squares and 4 surplus: 32 − 20 = 12). If anything in the toy is to play
  matter against antimatter it is these two excesses, and no test of that is designed. Idea, not a decision; no knob.
- 2026-10-18, the owner, on "opening the curled direction loses squares": "why does it need to lose squares we are not
  conserving points". *Ours, exact:* in the toy the points are fixed (N) and so are the links (six a point); squares are
  not conserved, they are counted, and the energy is the count. The squares an opening loses are the loops of four that
  run round the curled direction: a direction four around has one such square on every edge along it, and a direction
  six around has none, because a loop of six is not a square. At fixed N the slab 4 × L × L holds 3.25 squares a point
  (3 flat + the wrap squares, N/4) and every flat three-direction torus holds exactly 3, so the opening must lose N/4
  squares (16 each, 4N) while it sheds N surplus squares (4λ each, 4λN): the release 4(λ − 1)N is the difference. Her
  point is right one level up: if points are not conserved, the total square count need not fall. A 6 × L × L flat torus
  holds 18L² squares against the slab's 13L²: the wrap squares are still destroyed, but the new points bring new squares.
  The toy cannot do this (every move is a switch of two links at fixed N), and a varying count is claim 3's parked
  decision (it needs a cost per point, the cosmological-constant term of dynamical triangulations). What follows for the
  curl-only store: the strict rule of Update 60 is written on the toy's moves, where opening always lowers the count. If
  the count may grow, "pays only moves that do not lower the square count" would let a store pay for an opening that
  adds points, and the rule would need rewriting for a growing toy before it meant anything there.
- 2026-10-18, the owner, on the toy conserving points and links: "but maybe we should be pricing cubes not squares".
  *Ours:* looked at the same hour (`sandbox/LOG.md`, 18 October; exact counts, one size each). Cubes per point run
  1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5 up the ladder (flat, slab, rod, 6-cube), so curling adds cubes as it adds squares and opening loses
  them: a curled direction four around holds more small closed loops of every kind, and whatever small closed thing is
  priced, opening it loses some. On the single moves a cube count runs the wrong way for her sentence: the slab's opening
  move destroys 14 to 16 cubes, a breaking move 6 to 8. What a cube price would change is elsewhere: the energy would no
  longer be a sum over edges (a cube has twelve), so defects would stop being exactly additive (O22) and a growing patch's
  energy could take a different shape from T54's quadratic; that is untested and is not the single-move question. In two
  directions the published energy already prices the top cell (the square); in three it still prices squares because that
  is what the Ollivier curvature counts, so a cube price is a new knob with no published source (S1) and would be our
  family, never the published model. Idea, not a decision; nothing registered.
- 2026-10-18, the owner: "And let's think of the end [N] is not conserved, but anyway the spirit is to move, but we have
  60% more volume". *Ours, exact (the slab 4 × 6 × 6 growing into 6 × 6 × 6 with its cross-section kept; counts by
  `sandbox/cube_price.py`):* points 144 → 216, half again as many (50 %, not 60); squares 468 → 648, so the count rises
  and nothing need be lost once points may appear; surplus squares 144 → 0; cubes 216 → 216, exactly conserved. The
  cube count is conserved for four around growing to six around and for no other width (8 × 6 × 6 has 288), and six
  around is what the tube opened into in two directions (O116). The energy: flat space has H = 0 at every size, so the
  release is 4(λ − 1) per slab point whether the points are kept or grow; what a growing count has no answer for is how
  many points appear, since the energy is indifferent to the size of flat space, and the toy has no move that adds a
  point (every move is a switch of two links). Both are claim 3's parked decision (a cost or credit per point) and
  Update 28's other model. Idea, not a decision.
- 2026-10-18: T69's held-out size read (O135, addendum): AS PREDICTED at lambda = 1.10 and 1.40; at 1.25 the budget ran
  out at saddle 62, two below the predicted 64, so that cell is untested with nothing against the prediction. The price
  of a clean opening is 64 at both sizes where reached, not the single-move wall. Her PAYS is unsupported within the
  reach and not excluded above it; the second stage (the slab's symmetries) is what decides.
- 2026-10-18, the owner: "No, I feel like I wanna keep using the Square counting classical methods, but I want us to have
  an open mind about how we can get this curled slab to open into three dimensional space I know we want to use the
  square counts and math but when I visualize it, there's basically a closed point at the convergence of the
  three-dimensional whirlpool. All the energy is directed right at that point the only thing the energy can be used for
  is curling and curling so eventually it has to curl and I don't understand what breaking squares is we are trying to
  think about what actually can happen inside a black hole." *Ours:* (1) What breaking is, in the toy: at a flat point
  the six links make fifteen pairs; the three opposite pairs are the directions and the other twelve each close a loop
  of four. A curled point has one of the opposite pairs closing a loop of four as well (the direction four around).
  A broken point has some of the twelve closing nothing: its neighbours no longer sit as a lattice. Every move is a
  switch of two links and does one of three things to a point: curls it (a loop of four appears on an opposite pair,
  a surplus square, charged 4(λ − 1)), opens it (that loop goes, paying the same back), or breaks it (a loop among the
  twelve goes, charged 16, and nothing is curled). From flat space the cheapest move breaks (64, four squares lost, no
  surplus) and every move that curls a point also breaks squares beside it (84 and up: four lost, four surplus). There
  are far more ways to break than to curl (O20), which is why T21, T26, T27, T34, T42 and T57 broke and nothing folded.

- 2026-10-18, the owner: "I thought I read there was matter and antimatter at the start and they canceled out? They
  they even know something of proportions?" *Ours; settled cosmology from general knowledge, to verify with a physicist
  and against [Planck18] before any of it is cited:* yes to both. The hot early universe held matter and antimatter in
  almost equal amounts; as it cooled below the temperature at which pairs could be made, they met and became light, and
  what is left is the excess. The proportion is measured as the number of ordinary-matter particles per particle of light
  today, about six in ten billion (6 × 10⁻¹⁰), from two independent routes that agree: the amount of deuterium made in the
  first minutes, and the pattern of the cosmic microwave background. Read backwards, for roughly every billion
  matter-antimatter pairs that cancelled there was one unpaired matter particle. No bulk antimatter is seen anywhere now
  (the antiparticles in cosmic rays are made in collisions). The excess itself is unexplained: making it needs three
  things at once (a process that changes the matter count, a difference in how matter and antimatter behave, and a moment
  out of equilibrium: Sakharov's conditions), and the known differences in the standard model are far too small. The
  record already uses this (Update 41: ordinary matter is "the billionth-part left when matter and antimatter
  cancelled"; Update 42: a sharp front supplies the out-of-equilibrium moment). *What it does to her idea of tonight:*
  if antimatter were the threshold payment and matter the release less that payment, the excess fraction would be
  (release − payment)/release ≈ 6 × 10⁻¹⁰, so the payment would have to equal the release to one part in a billion. In the
  toy nothing ties them: the release grows with the region (4(λ − 1) per point) while a barrier is a fixed number, and under
  tonight's rule the seam payment ran 4 per point against a release of 4(λ − 1), equal only at λ = 2, where the slab is no
  longer stuck (O41). Ours, from a look at two sizes, not a measurement; a close number would not be a result (rule 13).

- 2026-10-18, the owner: "6 is the minimum to convert, but curling energy could have applied within the horizon
  bringing a 100 or 10 or 8 down to 6 then 4". *Ours, exact where marked:* (1) At a fixed count of points, shrinking
  one direction stretches another: 8 × 6 × 6 and 6 × 6 × 8 are the same torus (288 points), and the slab it can curl
  into is 4 × 6 × 12; from the cube 8 × 8 × 8 (512) there is no six-around torus at all, and the only curl is straight
  to the slab 4 × 8 × 16. So her ladder of widths is a volume-preserving reshaping, her whirlpool, and which rungs
  exist is set by the point count. (2) Every flat torus sits at H = 0 whatever its widths (checked: 8 × 6 × 6, 6 × 6 × 8,
  8 × 8 × 8, 10 × 6 × 6 all 0), so "100 down to 8 down to 6" costs nothing at the ends and has no drive either way; it
  is a walk among degenerate flat states with seams between them that cost. Only the last step, 6 to 4, changes the
  books: the slab holds a quarter square and one surplus square more per point than any flat torus (3.25 and 1.0 a
  point against 3.0 and 0), and lies 4(λ − 1) per point above it (288 units at λ = 1.25 for 288 points). (3) The strict
  curl-only store of Update 60 (pays only moves that add surplus without lowering squares) can pay for that last step,
  since the curl adds both; it cannot pay for the reshaping steps, which add no surplus and lower squares at every seam
  along the way. A no-break store could pay them if the reshaping seams leave no point broken, which is unknown. (4)
  The measured record on widths runs against a drive to narrow: six around stays six around (O116), trapped heat does
  not widen a tube (T61), widening needed a price on loops of six and still left flaws (T62, T64), and no run has ever
  narrowed a direction at λ > 1. The one setting in which flat space curls by itself is λ < 1, where curling pays and
  flat space is the unstable state (Update 8's rehearsal), which is the wrong way round for X. So her picture needs
  either curling energy spent on the reshaping and the last curl (a feed into the fold, never tried: every fold test
  gave energy of the ordinary kind, and it broke space) or a drive to narrow that the toy does not have at λ > 1. (5)
  Cheap and exact, not done: T69's search run the other way, from the flat 6 × 6 × 8 to the slab 4 × 6 × 12 through clean
  states, which prices her last step's path; and a sandbox look at a flat torus fed curl-only energy at every point (the
  strict rule) to see whether anything curls. Idea; nothing decided or registered.

  rule for which pairs of points may merge ("the two types of points that can connect": the two sides of the bipartite

- 2026-10-18, the owner: "So antimatter builds in black hole until front meets threshold then everything outside of that
  energy release cancels leaving net matter." *Ours:* (1) Her sentence is, almost word for word, the ledger of the clean
  path measured this morning. As the front advances the stores pay 4 per point opened (the climb, λ-independent); at
  completion the direction's surplus is shed and 4λ per point comes back to the stores; the two cancel to within 4(λ − 1)

  outside that energy release cancels, leaving net matter" has an exact counterpart: the climb is repaid at completion
  and only the stored curling cost remains. What differs from her words: in the toy the "antimatter" (the climb) builds
  along the front during the opening, not in the hole before it; and whether it is called antimatter is a name, not a
  measurement. (2) The ratio this ledger gives is net over gross = 4(λ − 1) / 4λ = (λ − 1)/λ, exact for the fixed-count
  path. At λ = 1.25 it is 0.2. For it to be six parts in ten billion, λ would have to sit at 1 + 6 × 10⁻¹⁰: X all but
  degenerate with space (the tie of Update 5), stable for ever (the wall about 48), and a lump a billionth of the energy
  that passed through. That is her Update 41 picture in one number (the hot lump nearly everything, matter a sliver), and
  it is where she said she expected to work (λ about 1.02, Update 41). **But rule 13 applies with full force:** λ is the
  one free constant, so reading η = (λ − 1)/λ off the sky and setting λ from it is a determination, not a prediction. It
  becomes a prediction only if λ is pinned by something else first (the fertile window, the width of the product, the
  waiting-time law) and then lands near 1 + 6 × 10⁻¹⁰ unasked; and "the refund is the light, the net is the matter" is
  an interpretation of two numbers in a ledger, ours and unverified. (3) Under her contraction model the ledger is not
  yet known: if layers split with no seam there is no climb to repay, and the gross and net would both be the curling
  cost. (4) Nothing to run on this; it is arithmetic on the clean path plus a reading. Recorded as her claim in the
  form rule 11 asks: previous (Update 42) matter and antimatter as a question; now: the climb paid along the front and
  repaid at completion is the pair that cancels, the stored curling cost is the net.

  eight 6-cubes, 512 points, at g = 1.5 (O55, Update 27). It is not a constant of the toy: the balance moves with size

  smallest size and one temperature. (2) Why it resonated, our guess: it is the one place a calculation put a number

  over gross is (λ − 1)/λ, which at λ = 1.02 is 0.0196, one part in fifty, not one in a hundred million; one in a
  hundred million needs λ = 1 + 10⁻⁸, and the measured sliver (the baryon-to-photon ratio, about 6 × 10⁻¹⁰; general
  knowledge, to verify) needs λ = 1 + 6 × 10⁻¹⁰. Nothing in the record pins λ to within parts in a billion of 1; the
  counting balance is the only independent handle and it gives 1.02 at 512 points, drifting up with size. (4) Her
  logic is right in form, and it is rule 13's own: if something other than the sky fixes λ, then (λ − 1)/λ is a
  prediction and the sky tests it. What would have to be true: a quantity of the toy that pins λ independently
  (candidates in the record: the fertile window, the width of the product, the waiting-time law, the counting balance
  at the real size, which is itself a function of N and g and so needs the real N and g), its uncertainty propagated,
  and the neighbouring values tested. And one more condition that the contraction model raises: the ratio exists only
  in the local form (a seam paid along the front and repaid at completion); in the layer form gross = net and there is
  no ratio to compare. Idea, recorded; nothing to run until λ has an independent handle.

  to verify); a toy counterpart would need a quantity of two signs whose sum is what is conserved, which is a new
  ingredient under S1 and is not built. What the toy can offer is the split she is pointing at: shaped energy (the curl,

  up** is exact in the toy (the ladder 10 → 8 → 6 costs nothing; LOG, 18 October), but the toy's "flat" is a count at a
  point (every link carrying its full number of squares, d = 3; O116), not the cosmologist's flat (no spatial curvature,
  Ω = 1). A torus has no curvature by construction and the toy has no horizon and no expansion, so it has no flatness
  problem to solve: in this energy nothing wide can be curved. Her observation is right about the toy and does not carry
  to the sky. (2) **The 4 → 6 step as the fast expansion, the release at 6:** under the layer form the step is one move,
  it releases exactly 4(λ − 1) N_slab into the stores, and no later widening releases anything (exact). The shape is
  inflation's reheating (energy stored while the geometry changes, released as a hot lump when it ends), and the
  mismatch is size: one step of 50 % in one direction against at least e^60 in all three (general knowledge, to verify).
  (3) **"Expanding sedately as forces balance":** exact in the toy. With layer moves alone the width's distribution is
  Boltzmann in μ_N N (addendum to Update 65), so at μ_N = 0 the width random-walks between six and the wall with nothing
  driving it either way; "forces balance" is μ_N = 0. An accelerating phase would be μ_N < 0, a reward per point, which
  is a knob (S1) and the side on which a varying count runs away (the dynamical-triangulations note of today). (4)
  **Repulsive, then gravity wins, then something else:** this is the standard history (inflation; then radiation and
  matter slowing the expansion; then dark energy speeding it up again since about five billion years ago; general

  the toy has none of it. (5) **Scraps as the seeds of structure:** consistent with her lean that the scraps are the dark
  matter (Update 52), since in the standard picture dark matter's overdensities are where galaxies form. One way it can
  be wrong, exact and cheap to check: the scraps are knots of fixed energy at a number set by the opening (O107); knots
  scattered at random with equal energy make shot noise (a white spectrum), while the measured primordial spectrum is
  nearly scale-invariant (n_s ≈ 0.965) and correlated on every scale (Planck 2018; general knowledge, to verify). The
  saved T51 sheets (`results/t51_*_adj`) hold the knots' positions; whether they are Poisson or clustered along the
  seeds' boundaries is a sandbox look not yet run. (6) **"You can't use up the fuel; a machine, not a womb":** exact in
  the toy, and double-edged. The split credits the stores with exactly the fold's price, so the fold can be paid back at
  the next proposal; under a uniform leak any feed strong enough to pass the "whole cost" gate (r/ℓ ≥ 2a) can pay the
  fold back (r/ℓ ≥ a). Energy is conserved to the last unit, so the cycle makes nothing: the machine runs on the feed.
  The same fact is why the product does not stay open unless the lump leaves, which is the feed-after-the-burp choice

  count, Update 59). "Inevitable" is eternal inflation's claim (Vilenkin, Linde; general knowledge, not read), which

  apart galaxies. Breaks apart all matter. Vacuum decay. So 10^1000 is really the max. In way less than that an observer

  billion years left of good observational science."
  *Ours: this is piece 12's function (Update 43: observers who evolved per turn, times the branching), written out in
  stages for the first time. Nothing here is a knob or a run; the toy has no stars, no observers and no expansion. The
  standard numbers, from search summaries and general knowledge, all to verify
  (`docs/reading/notes/2026-10-18_far_future_and_observation_window.md`):* (1) **The eras.** Star formation is already past
  its peak (about ten billion years ago) and is nearly over by about 100 billion years, as she says; but the last small red
  stars burn for about 10^14 years, so the era of starlight ends there, not at 10^11. Then stellar remnants to about 10^39,
  then black holes evaporating, the largest gone by about 10^100 years. **10^1000 is not a milestone anyone has named**; if
  her conjecture of 6 October (no mass, no time; Update 44, now worded as the last black hole dissolving, Update 59) sets
  the end of a turn, that end is near 10^100 years. (2) **Observers per planet.** Earth is about 4.5 billion years old and
  stays habitable for complex life for roughly another one to two billion, not ten; a planet round a small red star could
  stay habitable for trillions. One technological society per planet lifetime is the only data point there is, and it
  cannot be read as a rate (it is the sample that had to exist to ask). (3) **The observation window.** Her "10 to 15
  billion years left of good observational science" has a published form: Krauss and Scherrer (2007) argue that in the
  standard model every galaxy outside our Local Group passes beyond the horizon and the cosmic microwave background
  redshifts out of reach, so a future observer sees one island of stars, infers a static universe and cannot infer a Big
  Bang. Their scale is of order 100 billion years, so her number is about ten times too early; the shape of her point
  stands. (4) **Heat death against big rip.** A constant dark energy (w = −1) gives her slow heat death; a phantom one
  (w < −1) gives a big rip in finite time. Her argument that the measure prefers no rip is a selection argument of the
  kind the record already holds (Update 43: the yield counts observers who evolved; Update 59: the branching count): a rip
  cuts the black hole era short, so a universe at w ≥ −1 births more daughters. Two cautions. The count of black holes is
  mostly fixed in the first tens of billions of years (structure stops forming once dark energy dominates), so heat death
  buys time, not many more black holes; and the current data (DESI DR2 and supernovae, 2025 to 2026) weakly favour a dark
  energy that was phantom in the past and is weakening now, at one to two sigma, which is neither of her two cases and
  would also avoid a rip. (5) **Vacuum decay** is a third ending, separate from both: the Higgs field dropping to a lower
  state at a random time, a bubble spreading at nearly light speed (the [C77] picture claim 4 borrows). Nothing measured
  dates it. (6) **The hotspot.** Her "longest observer evolution hotspot" is Dicke's argument (Update 26) applied to the
  whole era of starlight: observers sit where stars burn and planets last, which is now, early in a 10^14-year era. What
  the toy can say about any of this: nothing. What the record can do: write piece 12's function with these stages as its
  terms (the length of the era of starlight, the number of black holes formed, the observation window) so that a setting
  of the loop can be scored, which is the exact item Update 59 left open.

- 2026-10-18, the owner, on method: "So the toy does not explain flatness. It assumes it. So can't we say that because we
  know it's flat from measuring it is acceptable to use this toy? The point of the toy is to be the most simple
  mathematical description that describes what we know about reality. That is a useful thing to build, once it's built
  the best it can be, we can use it to make predictions." / "has a degree of freedom the toy lacks: a curvature radius
  for the large direction - should we add this on before making any predictions with it, then?" / "Flat in the toy says
  nothing about size. That's fine and good we can paste on other scientists' size stuff, as needed for predicting."
  *Ours:* (1) Yes, a measured fact may be an input; the price is that the toy can then never be scored on it, and
  flatness must not be counted among the things the toy got right. One caution with the words: the toy's "flat" (every
  link full, a count at a point) and the sky's "flat" (a curvature radius far beyond the horizon) are not the same
  quantity, so the match is of words, not numbers (rule 13). (2) No curvature radius before predictions: add a degree of
  freedom only when a registered prediction needs it. In this family nothing wide can be curved at the floor, so a
  curvature radius would be a new ingredient (a different lattice or a term that charges or rewards curvature), a knob
  under S1, and it would buy nothing for the questions the toy is built for (the ledger, the gate, the timing, the
  daughters per unit fed, the knots). (3) Pasting other scientists' results on is how piece 12's brief already works
  (the toy supplies one term, b; the rest is published). The discipline it needs: at every join, say which number is the
  toy's and which is pasted, and keep the list of what was put in (flatness; three directions; the curling cost; the
  gate; one-way daughters; the slab as X) apart from what came out (the lump a per point; the knots and their sizes; the
  converter; b = energy in over the lump), so that no prediction is scored on an input (rule 13: inputs checked for
  containing the target). On "once it's built the best it can be": the toy already predicts (T71's words are written);
  the project's way is a registered prediction at each stage, not one after a finished build.
- 2026-10-18, the owner, on the converter reading of Update 66 ("the parent makes nothing, it passes the feed through"):
  "Yes this makes sense." *Ours:* recorded as her acceptance of the reading.
- 2026-10-18, the owner, choosing piece 12's combination rule (the brief's section 4): "Per universe only ignoring the
  branching when we are thinking about identicle universes that can only be differentiated by a particle or quantum
  measurement that makes something different at the macro scale. 2 for predictions of when an observer is most likely to
  occur in time where t=0 and the end of life supporting stars." *Ours:* a decision (VISION Update 67). Her first rule is
  the one that avoids the trap O101 named: universes of one setting that differ only by a quantum outcome are counted each
  as a whole, so no exponential weight favours the deepest generations. Her second rule, read as counting observers across
  the tree, carries a known consequence (the youngness pressure of eternal inflation, [Freivogel11]; general knowledge, to
  verify): with daughters born at a steady rate, the universes alive at any one moment are mostly young, so the prediction
  for "when" is pulled toward the earliest time observers can exist; with "trillions" of daughters per universe within
  about 10^10 years that pull is strong, and we are at 1.4 × 10^10 years. Written out in the brief with the escape her
  first rule provides.
- 2026-10-18, the owner, on piece 14's open question (whether the versions are Everett's branches): "I believe they are as
  needed for amplitudes and interference to work correctly." And on the youngness consequence of her rule (ii): "Okay my
  first instinct is to say we don't count across the tree then the rate and order doesn't matter as we can line up all the
  universes aligning big bang time. But I need to understand this better: most universes alive at any moment are young, as
  in my scenario yes they are increasing exponentially, but when we make a prediction of when we are I'd want to look from
  outside of time: are you sure that forwards weight can't be balanced by the universe's last observer being way out at
  10^100 years? And red draws being stable for way longer than our type of star and there being more of those in the
  future?" *Ours:* (1) Her belief on the branches is recorded as her position; the brief's rule (i) now says the copies
  carry Born weights on her word, and O125's "untested" stands until piece 13's toy tests it. (2) Late observers cannot
  balance the weight; late births can. The across-the-tree count weights age τ by e^(−γτ), and γ is set by when daughters
  are born, not by when observers live: with 10^12 daughters born within 10^10 years, γ ≈ 2.8 × 10^−9 per year, and an
  observer at 10^12 years (the red dwarfs' era) would need 10^1180 times more observers than now to balance it, at 10^100
  years 10^(10^91); no finite universe holds them. What lowers γ is the birth time: the same 10^12 daughters born at 10^12
  years give e^(−0.4) at our age, nothing. And early births set a floor whatever comes later: more than about three daughters
  per universe within the first 10^10 years already costs e^(−1.4) at our age, a million costs e^(−18). So the across-the-tree
  reading survives only if the black holes formed early birth almost nothing and the daughters come from what falls in late,
  which under Update 66 (b proportional to the energy fed) is not what the toy says. (3) "Line up all the universes at their
  big bang" is the per-universe count by age, and it is what "from outside time" gives: the whole tree counted per universe
  has every universe with the same distribution over its own age, so the branching adds copies and no weight. The
  across-the-tree count needs a common clock across universes, which her hypothesis denies (time emerges inside each open
  space, Update 65; a daughter's clock is not the parent's). Her instinct is the reading consistent with her own picture,
  and it is the one eternal inflation's youngness paradox is an argument against the other (a regulator by a global time;
  [Freivogel11]). (4) Her red-dwarf point does bite, in the per-universe reading and against her own age the other way: with
  habitable planets round 0.1 solar-mass stars, the chance of an observer as early as now is at most about 0.1 % ([LBS16],
  read in full on 12 October; `src/orh/cosmic.py`), so a per-universe count predicts an observer at about 10^13 years unless
  red dwarfs are poor hosts or late observers are weighted down. Her own observation-window term W(t) (Krauss and Scherrer,
  about 10^11 years, not read) does the second: observers who cannot learn their universe's history count less, so the peak
  moves inside 10^11 years and our age is unremarkable. That makes the window a load-bearing choice, not a flourish: drop it
  and the per-universe count predicts the red-dwarf era. (5) A "balance" in the across-the-tree reading, the youngness pull
  against the red dwarfs' numbers, would need γ of a few × 10^−11 per year, two knobs set to land on our age; rule 13.
  Nothing here is a run or a knob of the toy.
- 2026-10-18, the owner, on the escape from the youngness weight: "And also that the black holes birthing now may still be
  running in 10^50 b years?" and, on "set by the mass that falls in over the long black hole era rather than by the holes'
  formation": "yes this makes sense". *Ours:* recorded as her lean toward late births, not as her word on which reading rule
  (ii) means. Two things stand against it, both from outside the toy. (1) Under the converter she accepted (Update 66) a
  black hole births only while it is fed, and the published far future has the feed front-loaded: nearly all the mass that
  ever falls in does so in the first 10^10 to 10^11 years (stars collapsing; the central black holes growing, mostly in the
  first few billion years); one late tranche follows at about 10^20 years, when galaxies relax and a minority of their
  remnants fall into the central hole while most are ejected; after that nothing is left to fall in (protons decaying from
  about 10^37 years, if they do), and a black hole loses mass by Hawking radiation faster than it gains it from the cold sky,
  the stellar ones gone by about 10^67 years, the largest by about 10^100 (Adams and Laughlin 1997, search summaries only;
  general knowledge, to verify). So at 10^50 years the holes exist but are fed nothing, and under the converter birth
  nothing. (2) Even granting late births, the early floor is set per universe, over all its black holes together:
  γ ≥ ln(B_early)/10^10 years. With about 10^20 black holes formed in the first 10^10 years, one daughter from each already
  gives e^(−60) at our age; 10^10 daughters give e^(−30); a thousand give e^(−9). The across-the-tree reading therefore
  needs the whole universe to birth at most a handful of daughters in its first 10^10 years, which with the lump per daughter
  proportional to the energy fed means a daughter costing more than almost any black hole holds by then. That is not
  "trillions"; with late infall a minority of a galaxy's remnants, the total would then be small too. So the three together
  (across the tree, trillions of daughters, the converter) do not fit our age under the published infall history; the
  per-universe reading, her instinct, needs none of this. The timescales above are a reading task before they are cited
  (`docs/reading/notes/2026-10-18_far_future_and_observation_window.md`).
- 2026-10-18, the owner, settling the reading of rule (ii): "daughter's clock is not her parent's - it's an exact copy
  though, starts at big bang runs though heat death, every time so yes The across-the-tree count needs a common clock

  the prediction forward (a rip at 30 Gyr puts a third of the count before today) but the data lean weakly the other way

  and it cuts against her own argument of this afternoon that the heat death is the branch with trillions of daughters;
  she cannot have both without a number. (2) Under a constant dark energy the vacuum does not pull galaxies apart: bound
  things (galaxies, the Local Group, solar systems) stay bound; what it does is carry everything else beyond the horizon,
  which shortens the window in which an observer can learn its universe's history. That is her W(t), already in the
  function: with a hard cut at 100 Gyr, 8 % of the count falls before today, unremarkable. So dark energy's measured effect
  is the shortener she already has, not a new one. (3) "No interaction between planets, no space wars" does nothing as a
  multiplier (a factor that acts on every time alike leaves the peak where it was), and does a great deal as a rule for who
  counts: count each civilization once at its origin, or give observers a short life per planet, and the count follows the
  planet-formation rate, peaks near 5 Gyr and puts over nine tenths before today. That is piece 14's known lever (the
  reference class; Hartle and Srednicki), and it overshoots: it makes us late rather than early. (4) Rule 13 applies to all
  of them: a term added because it lands on our age is a fit. The two that stand on their own grounds are her window (an
  observer who can learn its universe's history, chosen before this question arose) and the smallest stars' habitability,
  which astronomy can measure; the reference class is hers to choose on its own grounds, and whichever she chooses, the
  function is not scored on our age.

- 2026-10-19, the owner, sharing a Smithsonian article: "new research says space growth possibly slowing?" *Ours (search
  summaries only; the proxy refused the article, the RAS release and arXiv; `docs/reading/notes/2026-10-18_far_future_and_observation_window.md`):*
  the article (November 2025) reports Son, Lee and colleagues (Yonsei), MNRAS 2025: the standardised brightness of type Ia
  supernovae correlates with the age of the stars that made them (5.5σ claimed); corrected for it, the supernova data line
  up with DESI's weakening dark energy, and they claim the expansion has already stopped accelerating. Rebutted in January
  2026 by Wiseman, Riess, Schmidt and others ("Still accelerating", arXiv:2601.13785, MNRAS 2026): the standard correction
  for the host galaxy's mass already captures the environmental dependence, with it no age dependence remains, and a
  supernova's progenitor is far younger than its galaxy; a reply from the Yonsei group in May 2026 ("Still non-accelerating",
  arXiv:2605.21586). Unresolved and contested; the mainstream reading is still acceleration; nothing here says space is
  shrinking, only that the speeding-up may be ending. What it would mean for her picture if it held, each a target and not
  a confirmation (rule 13): (1) dark energy as "the energy space itself keeps" is a constant, and a dark energy that fades
  needs a source that changes, which the toy has nothing for; the dark-energy target sheet of piece 15 already asks whether
  her dark energy should change with time, and this is the observation it would be scored against. (2) A fading dark energy
  means no rip and a longer era of structure formation, so more black holes form late: more daughters, later, which her
  measure likes. (3) The observation window W(t), the load-bearing term of piece 12 and 14 (Update 68), rests on a constant
  dark energy carrying other galaxies beyond the horizon in about 10^11 years (Krauss and Scherrer); if dark energy fades,
  the window may be far longer, and the per-universe count drifts back toward the red-dwarf era ([LBS16]), which would make
  our age early again under her own rule. So the result, if it stands, would weaken the term that rescued our age; that is
  the sharpest thing it says to her picture. Nothing recorded as a decision; nothing in the toy changes.
