- 2026-10-19, the owner, "the electromagnetism hypothesis": "curling energy is matter and uncurling energy is antimatter.
  There is a tiny bit more matter because in addition to the amount of the release the stores hold a little more: maybe
  it's what comes in during the 4 to 6 pre big bang transition? So plasma settles to the building blocks of both. then
  they cancel. Reversed values of certain quantum particles. When the canceling happens we get that full photon blast that
  lights everything, becomes the background radiation. / sufficiently energetic radiation can produce particle-antiparticle
  pairs, provided conservation laws are satisfied. This is what happened with the release: particles and antiparticles from
  energy only. / Uncurling energy is very energy dense. We know some fundamental processes treat matter and antimatter
  slightly differently. This is called CP violation - can we start exploring cp violation? / Curl and uncurl, a little more
  curl." *Ours.* Her earlier form of the same idea is the entry of 18 October ("antimatter is curling energy used to
  uncurl") with three facts against it as worded; this version moves the excess from "release minus threshold" to "what
  the stores hold beyond the release", and that is a different and better-defined claim, because the toy has that number.
  (1) **What is standard physics in it, and right as she says it** (general knowledge; the reading note has the sources):
  pair production from radiation above twice the rest energy, conserving charge and the rest; a hot early plasma with
  nearly equal matter and antimatter; their annihilation feeding the photon bath whose last scattering is the microwave
  background; the excess of matter about one part in 10^9 to 10^10 of the photons (the baryon-to-photon ratio, about
  6 × 10^-10); and that the known processes treating matter and antimatter differently (CP violation, seen in kaons since
  1964, in B and D mesons, and in a baryon for the first time in 2025) are far too weak in the Standard Model to make that
  excess. So the origin of the excess is an open problem (baryogenesis), and Sakharov's three conditions for solving it
  are: a process that changes the net count, a process that treats the two differently (C and CP violation), and a
  departure from equilibrium. One correction of detail: the annihilation light is not itself the background radiation we
  see; it was thermalised long before, and the background is the bath's light released when atoms formed, 380,000 years
  on. The excess is what survived; the bath carries everything else. (2) **Where her picture meets the open problem.** The
  burp is a departure from equilibrium, Sakharov's third condition, and the record already says so (Update 42: the
  best-studied mechanism makes the excess at the walls of a first-order transition's bubbles). Her "the stores hold a
  little more" supplies the asymmetry as an input (the second condition put in by hand), not as something the toy makes.
  (3) **What the toy has for "curl and uncurl, a little more curl", exact under the layer form (`sandbox/LOG.md`, 19
  October).** A contraction costs exactly a N_slab and a split releases exactly a N_slab, so curl and uncurl cancel to the
  unit: that is the reference arm of T71 (the lump paying the fold back). Under her one-way rule (Update 66) every daughter
  carries exactly a N_slab and nothing more, and the "little more" sits in the **parent**: it is whatever the stores hold
  when one looks, the gate plus the feed since the last burp. In one seed it read 0.3 % of everything released at
  λ = 1.25 and 28 % at λ = 1.10; a number set by the gate, the rate and the moment, not by the burp. Two things follow.
  The daughter, which is the universe that would have the excess, gets none under the current rule; for a daughter to
  carry "a little more", something of the parent's stores would have to go with the lump, which Update 66 forbids
  ("nothing can go from open space to the slab" is one way; this would be the other direction, and it is not decided).
  And the ratio is not 6 × 10^-10 and must not be made so (rule 13). "The 4 to 6 pre big bang transition": read as the
  fold 6 → 4, the only step of the ladder that costs (Update 65); what comes in during it is the feed, and under g1 the
  parent holds exactly one slab's worth more than it releases at the instant of each burp. (4) **CP violation in the toy:
  an exact no, with one door.** The toy's only two kinds are the two sides of the bipartite graph, every link joins one of
  each, and every point has the same number of links, so the two kinds are equal in number in every valid state at every
  size: an excess of one kind cannot exist (confirmed on the run's end states, 108 and 108). The energy is symmetric
  under swapping the two kinds, and so is every move (the contraction removes one of each). So the toy as it stands has
  no C, no P, and nothing that treats the kinds differently; "exploring CP violation" in it means a new ingredient under
  S1: a term or a move rule that tells the two kinds apart, or a degree rule that lets their counts differ. The one door
  that exists already: the exchange sign of Update 29 (O57) gives some arrangements an odd renaming and zero weight, a
  selection rule and not an asymmetry, but it is the only place the toy's weights ever see a sign. (5) **What can be done
  now without a knob:** the reading (`docs/reading/notes/2026-10-19_cp_violation_and_baryogenesis.md`, search summaries,
  the papers not read), so that the words are used as physics uses them, and the exact statement above. What cannot:
  any claim that the toy makes or explains an excess. Idea, not a decision; no knob; the question whether the stores'
  excess may travel with the lump is hers.
- 2026-10-19, the owner, passing on an explanation (drafted elsewhere; her words where quoted): "'Seeds' are the tiny
  differences in density that the early universe started with. 'Laid by something else' means the scraps can't be the
  source of that pattern, so some other process has to make it." The text sets out, as standard cosmology to check with a
  physicist, the one-part-in-100,000 density differences seen in the microwave background, their growth by gravity into
  galaxies and voids, and dark matter falling into the dense spots first because light does not push it ("scraps falling
  into seeds" means the scraps as the dark matter that collects in dense spots someone else made); then, marked "ours,
  from the 18 Oct look, not evidence", that knots scattered at random "make noticeable lumpiness up close, but it
  averages out quickly over larger regions, like a pinch of sand on a beach", so they give "far too little lumpiness at
  large scales" against the sky's nearly equal strength at every scale (tilt about 0.965); that inflation is the standard
  seed-maker and cosmic strings and other defects "were tested and mostly lost", "a caution for your picture: scraps are
  exactly that kind of leftover"; and two candidates inside her picture, untested: the burp starting at slightly
  different moments in different places, and quantum jitter in X before the opening, both facing the test that a process
  with a built-in size makes lumps of that size. *Ours, against the record and general knowledge, to verify with a
  physicist.* (1) So her question of this morning reads: are the primordial density differences the damage left where the
  room ran out? In the toy the damage is the knots, laid along the front as it passes (T51, T55), so the question becomes
  whether a sprinkle of knots laid by a front could carry the sky's pattern; the passed-on text answers no, and we agree
  with its conclusion, with two corrections to its reasons. (2) The "pinch of sand" argument as written is backwards for
  knots placed independently at random: such a sprinkle has density differences that fall as one over the square root of
  the count, slower than the sky's pattern, so it gives too much relative lumpiness at large scales, not too little (seen
  in the gravitational potential it diverges at large scales). The "far too little" is right for a different and
  stronger reason: knots made by a process that rearranges energy locally, as the front does, cannot make differences
  larger than the region the process has reached, and the published bound for any such causal, conserving process is a
  spectrum that falls far faster at large scales than the sky's (the k^4 bound of Zel'dovich and Peebles; general
  knowledge, to verify). The sky's differences are correlated across regions that had never been in contact at the time
  the light was released, which is the horizon problem, and inflation's answer is to stretch them from inside one region.
  (3) Cosmic strings and other defects lost on the microwave background not mainly through the strength at each scale
  (strings give roughly equal strength at every scale) but because defects keep making new differences at all times, so
  the sound waves in the early plasma are not in step and the peaks in the map are washed out, where the sky shows sharp
  peaks (COBE, Boomerang, WMAP, Planck; general knowledge, to verify). The caution stands and is sharper than stated: a
  leftover laid by a front after the opening is the kind of source that fails that test. (4) What the toy has for the
  two candidates. The first, the burp starting at different moments in different places, is what T37 and T53 show:
  several seeds in a long torus, fronts meeting; T50 measured the front's speed; the energy density at completion from
  several seeds is measurable in the toy at one size and would have the seed spacing as its built-in scale, exactly the
  test the text names; in the toy there is no expansion, so nothing stretches it. The second, jitter in X, has no
  counterpart: X is the slab, a uniform arrangement with nothing that flickers at fixed total energy except the stores,
  and the toy has no quantum rule for it (Update 57 adopted the amplitude rule for piece 9, not for X). (5) The one thing
  the text does not say: in the toy, the release itself is uniform, a per point, so the burp makes a perfectly smooth
  hot lump; the only differences it makes are the knots and the seams of the front, both laid behind the front. That is
  the exact statement of the problem for her picture. Idea and reading, not a decision; no knob; nothing measured.
- 2026-10-19, the owner, on paper 13's sentence that it "asks the loop only where the amplitudes live": "in the loop
  there are sets of universes that are identicle until each moment passes. From the macro perspective those versions are
  indistinguishable so we experience all of the set, until a measurement or some next moment locks us into one version."
  *Ours, unverified (general knowledge, to verify):* this is her Everett reading (18 October) stated as a place: the
  amplitudes live on a set of versions that agree on every record, and they separate when a record (a measurement, or any
  next interaction) makes them differ, which is decoherence. Two things the published rule adds, and paper 13 must say:
  until they separate the versions are not only all experienced but can cancel one another (interference), which is what
  counting alone could not give (T15 rung 3a); and "the next moment locks us in" holds only where that moment leaves a
  record, since a version change that leaves no record can still be undone (piece 13's toy, O125). What the toy still
  lacks is the object: which arrangement in it carries the versions (the loop of four is not a resonator; O30).
- 2026-10-19, the owner, a handoff of hers on the inflow and the primordial density differences (drafted in another
  conversation of hers, a spoken exploration, and passed on; kept as received in
  `docs/theory/omega_opening_inflow_density_handoff_2026-10-19.md`; it calls the hypothesis Omega). Her choices from it, as
  that assistant put them in order: the question is the primordial density differences of standard cosmology, not the
  toy's seeds of an opening, and the matter-antimatter excess is parked as a separate track; her candidate is that
  "additional energy continues to flow into the black-hole-associated curled region during the short interval in which
  its opening transition is underway", "spread throughout the curled structure, not injected through a single doorway or
  location", a contribution that "may be tiny compared with the larger stored energy released at opening", and the
  question is "whether slightly asynchronous entry and conversion could leave spatial variations in the daughter's
  initial energy or later matter distribution". Her order of events: one direction curled inside the black hole; what
  falls in is stored and "does not retain its particle identity"; "lambda is a universal model coefficient, not
  something that rises with energy"; the opening begins and "further energy continues arriving while the transition is
  still in progress", the region "opening almost all at once" with "a brief transition interval" allowed; "the entire
  curled region opens first", then pairs are made and annihilate in the daughter; the daughter is a finite space whose
  expansion is "stretching existing relationships, not additional spatial regions continually uncurling"; her "roughly
  one-in-a-billion" was said of the matter excess and is "a conceptual suggestion, not a calculated energy fraction".
  The document's own program: a symmetry control first (a uniform inflow must give zero contrast), then the spatial
  response to a uniform, a time-varying and a spatially variable inflow at the same total, then stretching, then the
  sky's criteria; and its first question back to her: after the opening, does every region stretch equally, or do the
  rules already allow different rates in different places?
  *Ours, against the record, with one look (`sandbox/inflow_density.py`; LOG, 19 October).* (1) **Where her sequence
  already is the toy.** Under the layer form she chose (Update 65) with the gate g1 (Update 69) the whole region opens
  in one move: "the entire curled region opens first" is what the toy does, the "transition interval" has no length,
  and the late inflow is exactly what the stores hold beyond a N_slab at the instant of the split, which the ledger look
  of this morning already read (0.3 % of the release at λ = 1.25, 28 % at 1.10; set by the gate, the rate and the
  moment). Under Update 66 none of it reaches the daughter: the daughter is a ledger entry of exactly a N_slab with no
  geometry, so **in the toy as decided the daughter has no spatial field at all**, and her question cannot be put to it.
  Two things would have to change, both hers under S1: something of the parent's stores would have to travel with the
  lump (Update 66 forbids the other direction and does not decide this one; the same door the CP entry of this morning
  found), and the daughter's space would have to be represented, which it is not. (2) **The symmetry control (her
  document's Stage 1), run on the kernel.** The slab 4 × 16 × 16 at λ = 1.25 fed uniformly, or uniformly in space and in
  bursts in time, reaches the gate with every store equal; the field's contrast is exactly zero and the split releases
  a N_slab to the unit. The layer form passes the control: symmetric inputs give a symmetric output. (3) **The grain,
  not the rate (Stages 2 and 3; exact arithmetic, confirmed by the kernel).** A feed arriving in deposits of size q at
  random points is a Poisson sprinkle: when the stores hold m per point the contrast per point is sqrt(q/m), over b
  points sqrt(q/(m b)), and the spectrum is flat. At the gate m = 2a, so at λ = 1.25 deposits of 1 give 0.71 per point
  (measured 0.69 to 0.74), deposits of 4 give 1.41 (1.41 to 1.47), deposits of 0.01 give 0.071 (0.069 to 0.073), and at
  four times the rate 0.67 to 0.73: **the rate does not enter.** A contrast of one part in 10^5 at the gate would need a
  grain of 2 × 10^-10 of a unit. The field is white: averaged over 4 × 4 columns it falls from 0.7 to 0.06 to 0.08, as
  1/sqrt(b) says. So a grainy inflow hands on its own grain, as her document warns, and the toy adds that the grain is
  the only thing it hands on. Under the reference rule f1 (the release credited to the two layers beside the cut, the
  new layers empty) the field after the split reads 4, 0, 0, 4, 2, 2 per layer: a pattern with the width as its scale,
  the "built-in size" her document cautions against, made by the move and not by the inflow. (4) **The front form (the
  2D tube 4 × 128 with a store at every point: the local form she did not choose, kept as the control), where the lump
  lands where the front made it.** Fed uniformly, every store reaches the wall of 12 in the same sweep and the tube goes
  everywhere at once (81 to 103 of 128 columns in one block of 50 sweeps): a uniform feed makes a flash, not a front,
  because the wall is a gate at every point and uniformity trips them all together. Fed in random deposits the Poisson
  tail trips a few points early (1 to 17 columns in the first block, at sweep 150 to 700 against 1,250), and a front
  runs from them. In both the product is hot and largely broken: 4 to 8 units per point are fed before the first seed
  can go, against a release of 1, because with one store per point nothing can start until the stores hold the wall,
  which is twelve times the lump; the inflow before the opening is never a sliver in this form (T9's "too little room"
  seen from the other side). The energy per column at the end has a contrast of 0.24 to 0.35 under every feed, uniform
  included, falls as 1/sqrt(b) (about 0.15 over 4 columns, 0.06 over 16, 0.03 over 32), has a flat spectrum (fitted
  slopes between −0.6 and +0.5, about zero) and no correlation with when the column opened (−0.07 to 0.15). So in the
  front form the control does not give zero, because the chain's own randomness (which point moves next) is an input
  her document's Stage 1 did not list, and what it gives is white noise with no scale beyond the column: the
  "microscopic fluctuations" candidate, in the toy, is the sampler, and it makes the same spectrum as the knots (the
  look of 18 October). (5) **What the toy cannot do (Stage 4).** Nothing stretches: the only move that adds space is
  the split, deterministic and uniform; stores exchange energy only through moves, so a deposited pattern neither
  mixes nor grows; there is no pull to amplify it and no expansion to carry it to large scales. Her first question
  (does every region stretch equally?) has no counterpart in the toy, and is hers to answer before anything is built.
  (6) **The rate's one role, exact.** In the front form the late inflow per point is r × T_front, the rate times the
  crossing time, against a release of a; "tiny" means r far below a / T_front. The crossing time grows with the tube
  (the speed per chain sweep halves as the length doubles, O93; fixed per fair sweep), so the admissible rate falls with
  the size of the daughter. The rate sets the size of the late sliver, never its pattern. (7) **Said plainly.** The
  inflow's unevenness, if any, is what the toy hands on; the toy makes no unevenness of its own except white noise
  from its sampler and its knots; and under the decisions in force the daughter has no field to hold any of it. The
  sky's pattern (nearly equal strength at every scale, in step across regions never in contact) is not white noise,
  and no feed can make it so here. Idea, reading and a look; no knob; nothing registered. Asked of her: the stretching
  question, and whether anything of the stores may travel with the lump.

  and so constant per volume (the dark-energy reading, Update 70). Open: where new points appear; if inside a flaw, the
  toy does not say what happens to it.

  early seeds with a nearly scale-free pattern, while random speckle adds a different, flat pattern whose size
  observations limit, more tightly the heavier and rarer the objects. So: flaws foamed at random while a daughter is
  young, then frozen and carried apart, with the clumps grown later by a pull the toy does not yet have (piece 8).

  terms. Standard relatives (general knowledge, to verify): like charges repel; exclusion keeps like particles apart.

  our space, which they do if our space is itself the edge (the growing surface of an opening). If every point can make
  new points at a fixed rate the growth is exponential, which is the behaviour of a cosmological constant; growth only at
  a boundary is slower. Dark energy speeds expansion up (in effect a push); matter's gravity slows it.

- 2026-10-20, the owner: "I'm thinking something about the bath creates flaws, no warm bath no flaws." *Ours, against the
  record:* so it is in the toy. A flaw costs energy (20 at least in the burp's product, 32 in a plain flat torus), so a bath
  makes them at a rate that falls as exp(−cost/g) and a cold one makes none (measured for the tube's wall, which follows
  that law with nothing fitted). Three routes to flaws, all warmth: the network's own missing squares at a coupling above
  zero (Update 70's dark-energy candidate); the knots an opening leaves, more of them the more seeds warmth starts (T37);
  and the burp's own heat, which leaves flaws when the surroundings cannot absorb it (T9, T61), so even a cold box gets a
  bath from the burp. Slow cooling heals some of them (the columns, T51). Standard counterpart (general knowledge, to
  verify): thermal relic dark matter, whose abundance is set by the temperature at which it stops being made and
  destroyed (freeze-out).

  farthest light we can receive cannot be seen, so observation does not rule it out, provided we are far from it. But
  growth at a far edge alone would not move galaxies apart here: Hubble's law says the space between us and every galaxy
  is growing, in proportion to the distance. Under Update 72 (space grows by gaining points, not by stretching), that
  needs new points throughout our space as well as at the edge. Also, the speed limit is local: in standard cosmology
  distant regions recede faster than light without breaking it.
- 2026-10-20, the owner: "about one per three seeds - this seems important. Could there be some mechanism in the (expanded,
  getting close to full) toy that sets the value?" *Ours:* the record's numbers: one seed leaves about one scrap (T10, 1.10
  per tube in T17); each extra planted seed adds 0.29 ± 0.04 (T17); natural seeds in long tubes leave 0.35 columns per
  seed (T37, twelve tubes at L = 1024, besides 15 other small pieces per tube). The published mechanism that sets defects
  per seed is Kibble's (general knowledge, to verify; Kibble 1976, Zurek 1985): separately started regions each choose
  one of a few equivalent ways to form, and where neighbours chose differently they cannot join cleanly, so the number of
  defects per region is set by counting the ways, not by tuning. In the toy a tube opens into six around with its seam
  shifted, which can happen in more than one way; whether the knots sit where neighbouring patches chose differently,
  and what the count of ways predicts per seed, is an exact question on saved end states (T17, T37). Rule 13: the 0.29
  and 0.35 are known, so a count that gives one third is a postdiction unless it also predicts a setting not yet run
  (another λ, or the slab's opening in three directions).

- 2026-10-20, the owner: "What is a compression wave?" and "How much canceled vs remained after the big bang". *Ours
  (general knowledge, to verify):* a compression wave squeezes and stretches the medium along its direction of travel
  (sound; an earthquake's P wave), where a shear wave moves it sideways and changes shape, not volume (an earthquake's S
  wave); gravitational waves are only the sideways, area-keeping kind. Nearly all matter and antimatter cancelled: today
  there are about 1.6 billion photons of the cosmic background for each proton or neutron (baryon-to-photon ratio about
  6 × 10⁻¹⁰), quarks and antiquarks cancelling about a millionth of a second in, electrons and positrons at about a
  second.
- 2026-10-20, the owner: "For gravity, what would be getting pushed t compress? Space is curving not pushing". *Ours
  (general knowledge, to verify):* nothing; that is why gravity has no compression wave. In Einstein's gravity moving the
  points of space around without bending it changes nothing physical, so a "squeeze" that is only a reshuffling of points
  is not a wave at all, and of the geometry's ten numbers only two can travel. Springs give a compression wave because
  they charge for stretch; an energy that charges only for curvature (Regge calculus: the deficit angles) does not.
  Recorded in the soft-space brief.

- 2026-10-20, the owner: "If points aren't real does that help? To make the springy links not compress?" *Ours (general
  knowledge, to verify):* yes; that is the principle. In Einstein's gravity points have no identity apart from the
  geometry (his hole argument, 1915), so how densely they sit is not physical and no compression wave exists. Regge
  calculus keeps a discrete trace of it: inside a flat region a point can be moved without changing any deficit angle,
  so moving it costs nothing (exact only in flat regions; Dittrich's work on discrete diffeomorphisms, not read by us).
  One consequence for her growth rule: if points are not real, a new row makes more space only if it brings new length,
  not merely new points. Recorded in the soft-space brief.

- 2026-10-21, the owner, on step 3 of the claim: "One potential issue is the amount in a black hole is already a small
  proportion of that universe, then the 1/1000000000 of that net matter is more tiny, but the daughter and mother
  universe are identical. Is that inconsistent or does it not matter for some reason like it is the relationships and
  proportions that are meaningful not the amount/ size of matter at birth. What do u think?" *Ours, unverified:* two
  parts. (1) The one-in-a-billion is a ratio inside the daughter (matter per unit of light at its birth), not a share
  of the parent, so the black hole being a small part of its universe does not shrink it; what has to be the same in
  every daughter is that ratio, which must then come out of the snap's own rules (in her hypothesis, how much of what
  the stores carry in is unbalanced, against the burp). In the toy as built the gate fires when the stores beside the
  cut hold the whole curling cost, so the stores at the snap are as large as the burp itself; the excess would need to
  be a billionth part of them that is not cancelled, not the stores' size. (2) The amount does matter for one thing:
  with energy conserved across the change (claim 6), a daughter starts with no more than its black hole converted,
  far less than our universe holds within its horizon, so to look like its mother it has to grow after the burp.
  General relativity allows that (an expanding space has no global energy book; inflation makes a large universe
  from a tiny patch: Farhi, Guth and Guven 1990, not read by us, to verify), so "identical" can mean the same rules and
  the same ratios, not the same total. That adds a requirement: something after the burp must expand the daughter,
  which nothing in the stiff toy does.

  opening). (b) Light does turn into matter under enough energy: two photons can make an electron and a positron
  (Breit and Wheeler, 1934; seen in heavy-ion collisions in 2021), but always as a pair, one of each, so it makes no
  excess by itself. (c) Her curvature route has a published cousin: gravitational baryogenesis (Davoudiasl and others,
  2004), where the changing curvature of an expanding space biases matter over antimatter; not read by us. (d) The end
  state: in a universe whose expansion accelerates, groups of galaxies are carried out of each other's reach, so the
  last black holes are many, each in its own region, each evaporating alone; the claim now says so.

  helium nuclei (two protons and two neutrons each; about a quarter of the mass), a little deuterium and lithium, and
  nothing heavier; neutral atoms only at about 380,000 years. The Big Bang is not an explosion into a cloud: space grows
  everywhere. The clouds, and every element heavier than lithium, come later from stars, which also make more helium (the
  Sun does now); the first stars burn out and explode in a few million years, seeding the clouds that make later stars.
  Earth's helium is mostly from radioactive decay in its rocks. A proton's mass-energy is about 1.5 × 10⁻¹⁰ joules; a
  nuclear bomb releases about a thousandth of the mass of its fuel, from vast numbers of nuclei. Stars above about 20
  solar masses end as black holes, and neutron stars that collide can make one; the Sun will end as a white dwarf.

In three dimensions the six face hypotenuses of one kind in a cube form a regular tetrahedron (Update 87, exact), whose smallest ring is a triangle of three; so the thing "3D opens up" is the 45° square becoming a tetrahedron, and her thirds (the three planes through the body diagonal at 60°, 22 October) exist only there. (2) In the toy, under the review's own way of making the graph a surface: a curled ring spliced into a flat sheet reads flat at its own points but makes the sheet into a surface with a handle, and the splice carries exactly 4π of saddle, whatever the curl's size (the built handles with 8 and 16 surplus squares both carry 4π). (3) The splice can be two-sided (a handle) or one-sided (a cross-handle), depending on how its ends are joined; her "saddle-shaped 2sided plane" picks the handle. What does not. (5) Exact: the toy has no sphere at any point: its graph has two kinds of point, so no face is shorter than a square, and every point reads flat or saddle (K ≤ 0). On a torus with no handle every point is then flat. Her "sphere" side lives only as topology in the stiff toy, and as a positive deficit angle in stage B.
